# F1 — Slide images: root cause, policy, verified picture library

Author: Agent F1 (overnight run, 2026-09-20). Scope: every slide picture in the lesson factory (generated decks), the 144 curated decks and the hand-built pilot.

Owner's words: "the images are not suited to the topics or key words … ITS INCREDIBLY IMPORTANT THAT THE IMAGE DOESNT CONTRADICT THE PAGE OR CONTENTS."

## 1. Root cause (why the art was wrong)

Old code: `server/src/oak/factory/emoji.ts` (deleted) — a first-match-wins list of ~130 regexes over the slide text (`/\b(volume|capacity…)/ → 🧪`, `/\b(square|area|cube…)/ → 🧊`, `/\b(fraction|half…)/ → 🍕`, `/\b(force|pressure…)/ → 🧲`, `/\b(change|cost|pound…)/ → 💷` …) with per-subject fall-backs (`Maths: 🔢 📐 🧮 ➕`, `French: 🇫🇷 🥐 🗼 💬` …). Findings:

| # | Cause | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | **Fall-back art**: a slide whose text matched no regex was padded with the first two subject-default emoji, so EVERY one of the 50,460 generated slides had art. "sphere" is not in any rule, so the owner's slide got `🔢📐` (number pad + set square) = `SUBJECT_DEFAULT.Maths[0..1]`. | 100% of slides had art; the sphere practice slide is exactly the reported 🔢📐 |
| 2 | **Metaphors as depictions**: pizza for "half", magnet for "pressure/force/speed", banknote for "change/cost/state", dragon for "quest/myth" (and any word containing them), ice cube for "cube/square number", petri dish for "experiment", abacus for "equation", bacteria for "health", microscope as the Science default. | sample table below |
| 3 | **Art picked per keyword, independently**: up to 3 emoji from 3 different words, so a panel is incoherent (`💷🧲🔬` on "Sublimation and deposition"). | slide 52 of the sample |
| 4 | **Language decks**: 🇫🇷/🇪🇸/🇩🇪 on 7,220 slides (14%) = the *language's* flag, not anything the slide names. Spanish lessons about Colombia, Guatemala (Rigoberta Menchú) and Mexico showed the flag of Spain; a lesson on Switzerland showed Germany's. | samples 28, 58, 59, 63 |
| 5 | **Art on question slides**: `practice` / `check` slides (13,976 = 28%) got art too, i.e. art beside the question it could answer or contradict. | kind counts |
| 6 | **Constant decoration**: ⭐🏅 on all 7,057 summary slides; the intro "cards" got their emoji from the same regexes (17,613 cards). | counts |
| 7 | **No verification at all**: the validator only checked `art.length <= 4`. Nothing tied a picture to the slide's text, and nothing could notice a contradiction. | validate.ts (old) |
| 8 | **Curated decks / pilot**: agents were free to write any emoji: 4,942 slide-art emoji + 1,238 card emoji in the 144 curated decks (`✅ 💡 📚 🤔 🎯 🔗 …` mostly generic; some wrong, e.g. `🌀` for "a fan", `🥚` for "egg cell"), plus 23 art arrays and 24 card emoji in the pilot. | audit below |

### Sample of 63 generated slides before the fix (Maths/English/Science/French/Spanish/German x KS1–KS4, random, seed 7)

Verdicts (my reading of each slide's text against its art): **wrong / misleading 26 (41%)**, **irrelevant or generic filler 35 (56%)**, **suited 2 (3%)** → 61 of 63 (97%) had wrong, irrelevant or misleading art. (Sample generator: slide list reproduced from the pre-fix decks; e.g. #1 "Representing longitudinal waves" 🔊🔬 (microscope); #2 "Motion calculations" practice 🧲📊; #3 "Radioactive half-life" 🍕🔬; #6 "Numerals 6-10" 🔢🧲; #11 imagery 🎵; #17 "c'est quoi" (cuddly toy, dog, bottle) 🎒; #20 partitive articles 💷; #23 imperfect tense ⚛️📊; #26 German questions 🐉; #28 Switzerland lesson 🏖️🇩🇪; #33 "More than and fewer than" ✖️; #38 special number sequences 🧊 (ice cube for cube numbers); #43 "Young animals" 🦊👨‍👩‍👧; #44 "Humans are animals" 🦊 (a fox); #45 "Different types of food" 🦠; #52 "Sublimation and deposition" 💷🧲🔬; #58 Colombia 🇪🇸; #59 Guatemala 🇪🇸; #63 Mexico 🇪🇸. Only #31 (product ✖️) and #34 (sum/difference ➕➖) were suited.)

## 2. New policy (enforced in code AND by the validator)

> A slide gets a picture ONLY when a verified match exists; otherwise NO picture. A missing picture is acceptable; a wrong or contradicting one is a severe bug.

Sources, in order:

1. **(a) Purpose-drawn library** `server/src/oak/factory/art/*` — 127 SVG diagrams (`library.ts`), each with exact `concepts` (whole words / whole phrases), `subjects`, optional `requires` (context words for polysemous concepts), `avoid` (other meanings / topics it would contradict), `numeric` (draws specific numbers), `family`/`covers` (pictures that exclude each other), a `doesNotShow` note and the verification evidence (section 4). Drawn with exact geometry (regular polygons from circumradius, cabinet-oblique 3D with hidden-edge rule, exact angles, exact reflection/rotation/enlargement maths, sine/cosine plotted every 3°, Snell's law for refraction …), theme-aware (CSS variables), light/dark.
2. **(b) Literal emoji** only from `allowlist.ts`: 129 concrete nouns whose emoji is an unambiguous depiction (🦷 tooth, 🧲 magnet, 🐕 dog…), by exact word (plus the same noun in French/Spanish/German for language decks). Ambiguous words are deliberately absent (bat, mouse-as-mouse-only-in-Science, seal, orange, plane, star, bank, rock, bulb, wave, heart, hand, foot, tea, scales, chips, crane, coat (animal coat), key, present, rose (past tense of rise) …). Context vetoes (`EMOJI_AVOID`): spelling bee, egg cell, pinhole camera, evolutionary tree, family tree …
3. **(c) Oak's own quiz images: NOT used.** Checked: 0 of 1,748 stem images sampled (Maths KS4 higher) carry alt text/metadata (`imageObject.metadata` is `{}`), so what an image depicts cannot be verified; they are question-specific diagrams ("find the angle x"); and putting them on a teaching slide would leak/duplicate the quiz answers. Rejected as unverifiable, documented rather than guessed.

Matching (`art/select.ts`) — never substring/fuzzy:
* whole-word/whole-phrase regex on the normalised slide text (plural of the last word only); quoted titles ("Small Island") removed; longest phrase first and consumed, so "right-angled triangle" never counts as "triangle", "square-based pyramid" never as "square" or "pyramid".
* **Prominence**: the concept must be in the slide TITLE, a key-word TERM or the LEAD, or (named in the lesson title and at least twice on the slide). A passing mention in a definition never earns a picture (this alone removed thousands of tangents: "circle" in the definition of "half turn", "flower" in a pea-flower genetics slide, "solid/liquid" in Earth's core).
* **Gates**: `subjects`; `requires`; `avoid` (word-start prefix or `=word` whole word; the LESSON and UNIT titles veto too: a "Sound waves" lesson never gets a transverse-wave picture); `numeric` pictures are refused when the slide text has any digit.
* **Family conflict**: every other member of the picture's family named in the slide must also be shown (max 2), else neither ("a cone is a third of a cylinder" → cone + cylinder; "hemisphere" never gets the sphere).
* **Never on a question**: `practice`, `check` and any slide with a choice/choices/match/sort/reveal/spell/lcwc/clap block get no picture at all (the picture could give away or contradict the answer). This alone fixes the owner's example: the "Which key word matches this meaning? A … is a 3D shape where every point on its surface is equidistant from the centre" slide has NO image; the sphere "Key words" slide shows a correct sphere.
* Max 2 pictures per slide; summary stars, decorative flags, and card emoji are gone (cards only get an emoji when the card TITLE is itself an allow-listed noun; otherwise plain text cards).
* Flags (`flag-*`) only when the slide title/lead names the country (France/Frankreich/Francia…; not "French"), never on history slides (war/occupation/division…).

Enforcement:
* `deckFor` (index.ts) runs `applyArtPolicy` on curated AND generated decks (the author's art is ignored), then `validateDeck` + **`verifyArt`** (independent check per slide: picture exists, slide is not a question, gates pass, a concept tag of the picture appears in the slide's own text, the picture is prominent, no family member is contradicted, emoji allow-listed and its word present, every card emoji depicts its own title). Any problem rejects the whole deck (falls back to the plain lesson). `cli.ts check` = **0 invalid**.
* The importer runs the same policy on the hand-built pilot deck.
* `npx tsx src/oak/factory/art/cli.ts check` — library self-checks (well-formed SVG, alt text, one owner per concept phrase and subject, no script, no NaN) + 32 policy unit tests (including the owner's sphere example, cone/cylinder, cube numbers, "non right-angled triangles", polysemy, history flags, spelling bee, egg cell …): 0 problems.
* Renderer: `features/learninghub/lesson/slides/SlideArt.tsx` (library loaded on demand; SVG inline with `role="img"` + alt text + caption; only verified ids render; unknown ids ignored), `SlideDeck.tsx` (a slide with no art has NO panel and a single-column layout — no empty box; on phones the picture panel sits under the text), `types.ts` (`Slide.pics`, cards' `emoji` optional, `normalizeSlides` keeps only `[a-z0-9-]` ids), `blocks.tsx` (cards without emoji), `SlideEditor.tsx` (ids/emoji are not editable text).

## 3. Results

All numbers are from the final code (`npx tsx src/oak/factory/cli.ts check`, 7,470 lessons, **0 invalid**).

| | Before (emoji heuristics) | After (verified policy) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Generated slides | 50,460, **100% with art** | 50,460: **2,485 with a verified image (4.9%)** = 2,314 library diagram only + 165 literal emoji only + 6 both; **47,975 without any image (95.1%)** |
| Lessons with at least one image | 7,112 of 7,112 | 1,076 of 7,112 (15%) |
| Practice / check slides with art | 13,976 (all) | **0** (never, by rule and by validator) |
| Summary stars ⭐🏅 / language-name flags | 7,057 / 7,220 slides | 0 / flags only where the slide names that country and no other (9 France, 7 Spain, 1 Germany, 1 Switzerland) |
| Intro/curated "cards" with an emoji | 17,613 cards, all | 251 (only where the card title is itself an allow-listed noun) |
| Curated decks (144 files, 2,216 slides) | 4,942 art emoji + 1,238 card emoji | 75 library pictures + 7 literal emoji + 52 card emoji (each verified); all rewritten in place by `art/fixCurated.ts` |
| Hand-built pilot (25 slides) | 23 art arrays + 24 card emoji | 0 (file cleaned; the importer also applies the policy) |
| Candidates REJECTED by the policy (slide had a concept match but no picture was given) | n/a | 7,393 slides had at least one rejected candidate: 3,130 other subject / other meaning of the word (e.g. Maths "reflection" vs light), 2,822 vetoed by an `avoid` phrase (other topic / would contradict, e.g. "non right-angled", "cube number", "frustum", "sound" lesson vs transverse wave), 2,687 only mentioned in passing, 1,164 polysemous word without its context word (e.g. "cell", "circuit"), 134 numeric picture vs slide with numbers, 74 another family member named (cone vs cylinder). Plus 13,976 question slides excluded up front. |

Most used pictures: fraction-wall 138, part-whole-model 137, mixed-improper 123, perimeter 114, decimal-place-value 110, story-mountain 107, handwriting-lines 100, changes-of-state 91, coordinate-axes 82, polygons 80, area 68 … (108 of the 127 pictures are used somewhere in the Oak library; the 19 unused — hemisphere, triangular-prism, tetrahedron, cube-net, right-angled-triangle, kite, octagon, ten-frame, hundred-square, scatter-none, tally, dice, analogue-clock, moon-phases, balanced-forces, em-spectrum, flag-austria/-belgium/-colombia — are correct and tested but no Oak slide currently names them prominently and safely enough). Emoji: 🏫 35, 👨‍👩‍👧 17, ☀️ 15, 🦷 14, 🧲 12, 🔬 11 …

### The owner's example, reproduced
* Year 11 "The volume of a sphere" → "Key words" slide: **sphere picture** (correct sphere, dashed equator) — tested in `art/cli.ts check` ("the owner's example: sphere key-word slide gets a sphere").
* "Key word practice → Which key word matches this meaning? A … is a 3D shape where every point on its surface is equidistant from the centre": **no picture** (question slide) — also tested.

### Visual review (real app, staging tenant `pnH8zTuvYlb7yJbvcanr`)
* All 19 subject x key-stage blocks (+ Science KS1) re-imported to the STAGING tenant only with `import.ts --slides` (two full passes: the second after fixes below). 0 failures.
* `e2e/f1-art-sheet.spec.ts` (run only through `scripts/e2e-locked.sh`): signs in as the staging tutor, opens sampled lessons, plays **Preview**, and for every sampled slide asserts what is rendered against what the generator produced (exact picture ids, alt text on every picture, inline SVG, **no art panel at all when there is no art (no empty box)**, no horizontal overflow, no page errors) and screenshots the slide. Run 1: 235 slides (91 with images / 144 without) across all subjects x key stages; run 2 (after fixes, new random seed): **231 slides (87 with images, 144 without), 230 asserted OK, 1 skipped (a different lesson with the same title was opened)**; mobile (390px) 31 slides OK. I looked at all 87 image slides (contact sheets) and 60+ plain slides.
* Problems I found by eye and fixed (round 1 -> round 2): family emoji on "word families" (vetoed); Spain flag on a "Spain and Cuba" slide (any other named country now vetoes a flag; history words too); bee emoji on "spelling bee" and egg on "egg cell" and tree on "evolutionary tree" and coat on animal coat (vetoed/removed); emoji named only once (e.g. the school emoji on a slide about verb endings) no longer shown (emoji now need two mentions); duplicate flag captions; overlapping labels/captions in ~20 diagrams (angle types, circle parts, parallel-line angles, water cycle, periodic table legend, activation-energy labels, right-triangle labels, Pythagoras formula, respiration/photosynthesis boxes, changes of state labels).

### Findings / limits (honest)
* Coverage is intentionally low (4.9% of slides): Maths and Science have most pictures; English has the story mountain and handwriting lines; French/Spanish/German get flags (only for a named country) and a few literal nouns. Everything else has no image by design. More library entries = more coverage (each needs a concept list, drawing and evidence row).
* 16 pictures have no Oak keyword with that exact name (checked in `scratch/oak-raw`); their evidence is curriculum knowledge (geometry facts stated per row). The KS1 Oak definition of "sphere" is "A 3D shape with one curved surface" (KS4: equidistant from the centre): both agree with the drawing.
* Some picture/slide pairs are correct but tangential (e.g. story mountain on "Writing part 2 of the climax", school building on "school" lessons whose text is about verb endings when school is in the title AND named again). None contradicts its slide in my review; I would rather drop more than risk one wrong.
* Picture captions are drawn inside the SVG and repeated as the figure caption for most pictures (deliberate: the SVG alone is self-explanatory when copied).
* Not drawn (would need careful anatomy/chemistry artwork I could not verify): human organs (heart, digestive system, skeleton), lab apparatus, molecules/bonding, electrolysis, rock cycle. They correctly get no picture.
* Oak quiz images (source c) are not used — see policy item 3.



## 4. Verification tables

Every library picture: the exact concepts it is valid for, the Oak keyword definition found in `scratch/oak-raw` (from a subject the picture is allowed in), how the drawing was verified against that definition/standard curriculum knowledge, and what it does NOT show. 16 pictures have no Oak keyword of that exact name ("curriculum knowledge"): the geometry rules are stated in the verification column.

| Picture id | Concepts (exact) | Subjects | Oak evidence (keyword definition found in scratch/oak-raw) | Drawing verification | Does NOT show |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `sphere` | `sphere` | Maths | **sphere**: “A 3D shape with one curved surface.” (Explore, recognise and compare three di…) | Oak keyword 'sphere' = 3D shape where every point on its surface is equidistant from the centre. Drawn: circle outline + equator ellipse (front half solid, back half dashed). | any other 3D shape; no measurements or numbers |
| `hemisphere` | `hemisphere` | Maths | curriculum knowledge (see verification) | A hemisphere is half a sphere (curved surface + one flat circular face). Drawn: dome (semicircle) sitting on its base ellipse, hidden back edge of the base dashed. | any other 3D shape; no measurements or numbers |
| `cylinder` | `cylinder` | Maths | **cylinder**: “A 3D shape with two circular faces and one curved surface.” (Explore, recognise and compare three di…) | Cylinder = two parallel congruent circular faces joined by a curved surface. Drawn: two ellipses + two vertical sides, hidden bottom back edge dashed. | any other 3D shape; no measurements or numbers |
| `cone` | `cone` | Maths | **cone**: “A 3D shape with one vertex, one circular face and one curved surface.” (Explore, recognise and compare three di…) | Cone = one flat circular face, curved surface, one apex. Drawn: apex, two slant edges, base ellipse (back half dashed). Never drawn with a second circular face. | any other 3D shape; no measurements or numbers |
| `cube` | `cube` | Maths | **cube**: “A 3D shape with six identical faces.” (Examine ways that cubes can be composed…) | Cube = 6 congruent square faces, 12 edges, 8 vertices. Drawn: front square, oblique back square of equal size; 3 hidden edges from the back-bottom-left vertex dashed. | any other 3D shape; no measurements or numbers |
| `cuboid` | `cuboid`, `rectangular prism` | Maths | **cuboid**: “A 3D shape with six rectangular faces.” (Explore, recognise and compare three mo…) | Cuboid = 6 rectangular faces, 12 edges, 8 vertices. Drawn: front rectangle 100x56, oblique back rectangle of equal size; 3 hidden edges dashed. | any other 3D shape; no measurements or numbers |
| `triangular-prism` | `triangular prism` | Maths | curriculum knowledge (see verification) | Triangular prism = 2 parallel congruent triangular faces + 3 rectangular faces. Hidden edges (only bordering hidden faces) dashed. | any other 3D shape; no measurements or numbers |

Only shown for the plain word 'pyramid' when no other base is named. | any other 3D shape; no measurements or numbers |
| `tetrahedron` | `tetrahedron`, `triangular-based pyramid`, `triangular based pyramid` | Maths | curriculum knowledge (see verification) | Tetrahedron = 4 triangular faces, 6 edges, 4 vertices. Convex quadrilateral outline, the diagonal through the far vertex dashed. | any other 3D shape; no measurements or numbers |
| `cube-net` | `net of a cube`, `cube net` | Maths | curriculum knowledge (see verification) | A cube has 11 different nets; this cross (1-4-1) is one of them. Six congruent squares. | any other 3D shape; no measurements or numbers |
| `faces-edges-vertices` | `face`, `edge`, `vertex`, `vertice` | Maths | **face**: “Any of the flat surfaces of an object.” (Identify 2D shapes within 3D shapes)<br>**vertex**: “A vertex is a point where two or more lines meet.” (Measure the size of angles accurately u…) | Face = a flat surface, edge = where two faces meet, vertex = where edges meet (corner). Cube drawn with one of each highlighted. Only used when the slide is about 3D solids with flat faces (no sphere/cone/cylinder, which have curved surfaces). Requires one of: 3d, 3-d, solid, cube, cuboid. | any other 3D shape; no measurements or numbers |
| `circle` | `circle` | Maths | **circle**: “A 2D shape with one curved line around the outside.” (Explore and recognise 2D shapes) | Circle = set of points at a fixed distance from the centre. Drawn: circle outline + centre point. | any other 2D shape; no measurements |

Nucleus drawn not to scale. Requires one of: nucleus, proton, electron, neutron, shell. Numeric: refused when the slide has numbers. | protons and neutrons individually; other elements; real sizes |
| `ph-scale` | `ph scale`, `universal indicator` | Science | **ph scale**: “The pH scale measures acidity and alkalinity and is often shown with values ranging from 1 to 14. A pH below …” (Acid rain and chemical weathering) | pH < 7 acidic, 7 neutral, > 7 alkaline (Oak KS3 'pH scale'). Colours are the usual universal-indicator colours (approximate). Numbers 0-14 shown. Numeric: refused when the slide has numbers. | specific acids or alkalis |
| `exothermic-profile` | `exothermic`, `exothermic reaction` | Science | **exothermic**: “a chemical process that transfers energy to its surroundings” (Aerobic cellular respiration in humans …) | Exothermic: products have LESS energy than reactants; energy released to surroundings; activation energy = extra energy needed to start (Oak KS3/KS4). | catalysts; numerical energy values |
| `endothermic-profile` | `endothermic`, `endothermic reaction` | Science | **endothermic**: “a chemical process that requires energy transferred from its surroundings” (Photosynthesis: an endothermic process …) | Endothermic: products have MORE energy than reactants; energy taken in from surroundings (Oak KS3/KS4). | catalysts; numerical energy values |
| `circuit-symbols` | `circuit symbol`, `circuit diagram` | Science | **circuit symbol**: “A circuit symbol is a simple picture or shape used to represent a component in a circuit diagram.” (Circuit symbols and diagrams)<br>**circuit diagram**: “A circuit diagram is a pictorial representation of a circuit.” (Circuit symbols and diagrams) | UK standard symbols (Oak KS2/KS3/KS4 electricity): cell = long thin line + short thick line; battery = several cells; lamp = circle with a cross; resistor = rectangle; ammeter/voltmeter = circle with A/V; fuse = rectangle with a line through it. Requires one of: circuit, circuits, current, electric, electrical. | LEDs, variable resistors, thermistors, LDRs |
| `simple-circuit` | `simple circuit`, `complete circuit`, `electrical circuit`, `circuit` | Science | **complete circuit**: “A circuit that has no gaps, allowing electricity to flow continuously around the circuit.” (Making parallel circuits)<br>**circuit**: “An electrical circuit is a closed loop or path that electricity can flow through to make a component work.” (Circuit symbols and diagrams) | A complete circuit is a closed loop of wire containing a power source (cell) and a component (lamp); a closed switch completes it (Oak KS2 'circuit'). Requires one of: current, battery, bulb, lamp, switch. | more than one component or branch; ammeters; voltmeters |
| `series-circuit` | `series circuit` | Science | **series circuit**: “an electrical circuit with a single loop” (Potential divider circuits) | Series = components one after another in a single loop, so the same current flows through each (Oak KS3/KS4). | anything not labelled |
| `parallel-circuit` | `parallel circuit` | Science | **parallel circuit**: “a circuit with separate branches connected to the battery” (Adding components to a parallel circuit) | Parallel = components on separate branches; current splits at the junctions (Oak KS3/KS4). | anything not labelled |
| `balanced-forces` | `balanced force` | Science | no Oak keyword with this exact name; the phrase “balanced force” is in 1 Oak lesson titles/keyword lists | Balanced = equal and opposite forces, resultant force zero (Oak KS3 'balanced forces'). Arrows exactly 50 units each. | the cause of the forces; friction; weight |
| `unbalanced-forces` | `unbalanced force`, `resultant force` | Science | **resultant force**: “the overall effect of the forces acting on an object” (Newton's Second Law) | Unbalanced = forces of different sizes; resultant force in the direction of the bigger force, 76 - 30 = 46 units to the right (Oak KS3 'resultant force'). | the cause of the forces; friction; weight; calculation |
| `transverse-wave` | `transverse wave`, `wavelength`, `amplitude`, `crest`, `trough` | Science | **wavelength**: “The wavelength of a wave is the distance a wave travels during one period.” (Measuring the speed of sound in air and…)<br>**amplitude**: “the amplitude of a longitudinal wave is the maximum distance each part of the wave moves forward or backward.” (Representing longitudinal waves) | Transverse wave: crest, trough, amplitude (max displacement from rest), wavelength (crest to crest); oscillations perpendicular to the direction of energy transfer (Oak KS3/KS4). y = 84 - 34 sin(2 pi (x-20)/100): crest at x=45, 145; trough at x=95, 195. | frequency, period or wave speed; longitudinal waves |
| `longitudinal-wave` | `longitudinal wave`, `compression`, `rarefaction` | Science | **longitudinal wave**: “each part of a longitudinal wave oscillates forward and backward in the same direction in which the wave move…” (Representing longitudinal waves)<br>**compression**: “part of a longitudinal wave in which parts of the wave are squashed closer together is called a compression.” (Representing longitudinal waves) | Longitudinal wave: particles vibrate parallel to the direction of travel, forming compressions (close together) and rarefactions (spread out); wavelength = compression to compression (Oak KS3/KS4). Displacement x = x0 + 8 sin(2 pi x0/80): compressions at 65 and 145, rarefactions at 25, 105, 185. | frequency, period, amplitude or wave speed; transverse waves |
| `reflection-of-light` | `law of reflection`, `angle of incidence`, `angle of reflection`, `incident ray`, `reflected ray`, `reflect`, `reflection` | Science | **angle of incidence**: “The angle of incidence is the angle between the incident ray and the normal.” (Refraction through a rectangular block)<br>**reflect**: “To reflect light is when light hits a surface and bounces off in another direction” (How we see the Moon from Earth) | Law of reflection: angle of incidence = angle of reflection, both measured from the normal (Oak KS3 'reflection'). Rays drawn at 40 degrees each side of the normal, exact. Requires one of: light, ray, rays, mirror, normal. | scattering; images in a mirror; curved mirrors |
| `refraction-of-light` | `refraction`, `refract`, `refracted` | Science | **refraction**: “Occurs when light travels from one transparent medium to another, causing a change in direction.” (Common defects of the human eye)<br>**refract**: “Waves are said to refract when they change direction upon entering a new medium.” (Light and colour) | Refraction: light changes direction when it changes speed at a boundary; entering a denser medium (air to glass) it bends TOWARDS the normal (Oak KS3/KS4). i = 50 degrees, r = arcsin(sin 50 / 1.5) = 30.7 degrees. | light leaving glass (bending away from the normal); lenses; prisms; total internal reflection |
| `magnet-poles` | `magnetic pole`, `north pole`, `south pole`, `like poles`, `unlike poles`, `opposite poles`, `attract`, `repel` | Science | **attract**: “To attract something is to make it move closer.” (Magnetic and non-magnetic materials)<br>**repel**: “To repel something is to make it move further away.” (Putting magnets together: attract or re…) | Like poles repel, unlike poles attract (Oak KS2/KS3 'magnets'). Top: [N S] then [N S] so S faces N (attract). Bottom: [N S] then [S N] so S faces S (repel). N red, S blue. Requires one of: magnet, magnets, magnetic, pole, poles. | magnetic field lines; electromagnets |
| `em-spectrum` | `electromagnetic spectrum` | Science | curriculum knowledge (see verification) | Order of increasing frequency: radio, microwave, infrared, visible, ultraviolet, X-ray, gamma (Oak KS3/KS4). Wavelength decreases as frequency increases. Band widths are equal (not to scale). | actual wavelengths or frequencies; uses of each wave |
| `story-mountain` | `narrative arc`, `story mountain`, `plot diagram`, `plot structure`, `rising action`, `falling action`, `climax` | English | **narrative arc**: “the structure and sequence of events in a story” ('The Twisted Tree': understanding narr…)<br>**story mountain**: “a way to sequence and order key events of a story to retell” (Sequencing and making a story mountain:…) | Narrative arc / story mountain (Freytag): exposition/opening, rising action, climax, falling action, resolution. Oak KS2-KS3 English 'narrative arc', 'climax', 'resolution'. Line rises to the single peak and falls symmetrically. Requires one of: story, narrative, plot, arc, novel. | specific story events; the exposition/conflict split of other models |
| `handwriting-lines` | `x-height line`, `x-height`, `baseline`, `ascender`, `descender` | English | **x-height line**: “this is the line that x-height letters reach” (The first join: aw, ap, an, ar with lea…)<br>**baseline**: “the tramline that most letters sit on” (Capital letters: A, H, K, T and S) | Handwriting guide lines: ascenders (b, d, h, k, l, t) rise above the x-height line, x-height letters (a, c, e...) sit between baseline and x-height line, descenders (g, j, p, q, y) drop below the baseline (Oak KS1 handwriting keywords 'x-height line', 'baseline'). Requires one of: letter, letters, handwriting, handwritten, write. | individual letters or how to form them |

## 5. How to re-verify

```
cd server
npx tsc --noEmit                                            # only the pre-existing curriculum/_check_ error remains
npx tsx src/oak/factory/art/cli.ts check                    # library self-checks + 34 policy tests -> 0 problems
npx tsx src/oak/factory/cli.ts check                        # every generated + curated deck through validateDeck + verifyArt -> INVALID 0
npx tsx src/oak/factory/cli.ts curated                      # 144 curated files valid
npx tsx src/oak/factory/art/cli.ts sheet [regex] [dark]     # HTML contact sheet of the library (/tmp/f1/sheet.html)
npx tsx src/oak/factory/art/cli.ts doc out.md               # regenerate the tables above
npx tsx src/oak/factory/art/fixCurated.ts [--dry]           # re-apply the policy to curated/*.json
npx tsx src/oak/import.ts pnH8zTuvYlb7yJbvcanr --subject Maths --keystage ks4 --slides   # STAGING only
scripts/e2e-locked.sh e2e/f1-art-sheet.spec.ts              # rendered check of sampled slides (F1_WIDTH=390, F1_THEME=light, F1_LIMIT=n)
```

## 6. Files

New: `server/src/oak/factory/art/{types,helpers,style,library,shapes3d,shapes2d,number,geometry,science,physics,english,allowlist,select,cli,fixCurated}.ts`, `e2e/f1-art-sheet.spec.ts`, `scratch/f1-art-samples.json`.
Changed: `server/src/oak/factory/{generate,index,validate}.ts` (no art in the generator; policy + verifyArt in deckFor; cards emoji optional), `server/src/oak/import.ts` (policy on hand-built decks), `server/src/oak/slides/spelling-words-with-the-suffixes-tion-and-cian.ts` (art/emoji removed), `server/src/oak/curated/*.json` (art rewritten), `features/learninghub/lesson/slides/{types,SlideDeck,blocks,SlideEditor}.tsx|ts` + new `SlideArt.tsx`, `docs/oak-import.md`. Deleted: `server/src/oak/factory/emoji.ts`.


## 7. X2 extension: verified Maths KS3-KS4 pictures (Y7-Y11, incl. GCSE foundation/higher)

Agent X2 (overnight run, 2026-09-20). Owner's brief: every page needs an appropriate image that never contradicts the page. Policy unchanged (verified concept match or NO picture); this section adds **110 new purpose-drawn diagrams** in `server/src/oak/factory/art/ext-maths-secondary.ts` (registered by one line in `art/library.ts`; tests in `art/ext-maths-secondary-tests.ts`, wired into `art/cli.ts check`).

### Before / after (Maths KS3-KS4, generated decks, 863 lessons)

| | Before | After |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Slides | 5,831 | 5,831 |
| Slides with a verified picture | 715 (12.3%) | **1,545 (26.5%)**; of the 4,239 non-question slides: 16.9% -> **36.4%** |
| by year (slides with a picture / slides) | Y7 166/977, Y8 182/1012, Y9 61/864, Y10 182/1644, Y11 124/1334 | Y7 244/977, Y8 268/1012, Y9 183/864, Y10 486/1644, Y11 364/1334 |
| Slides showing one of the 110 new pictures | 0 | 962 slides in 389 lessons; 103 of the 110 pictures are used by at least one Oak slide |
| (same measurement with the generator as of the end of the run, 5,213 slides after other agents' generator changes) | n/a | 1,464 slides (28.1%; 34.6% of non-question slides) |

Measurement (step 1): 3,524 non-question slides had no picture; the rejections that mattered were `avoid`/unit-title vetoes (903), `requires` (385) and "not prominent" (817), and the top un-pictured concepts were graphs (cubic, reciprocal, exponential, y = mx + c, proportion graphs, speed-time), standard form, surds, indices, sequences, probability trees, box plots / histograms / cumulative frequency, set notation, bearings, circle theorems, congruence, trigonometry ratios and rules, vectors, loci, solids with formulas, nets.

### What is different from the F1 library
* Every X2 picture is scoped `keyStages: ["ks3","ks4"]` (Maths only). A KS1-2 diagram of the same word is a different picture, so `art/select.ts` now builds the concept keys per key stage (a KS3-4 phrase such as "volume of a cone" must not consume the word "cone" that a KS2 picture needs) and the concept-owner self-check in `art/cli.ts` is key-stage aware.
* Pictures are GENERIC-correct: neutral labels (a, b, k, x, theta, n, r ...). The few that print a worked example (quadratic sequence 2, 5, 10, 17; nth term 3n + 1; factor tree of 60; stem and leaf; sharing 2 : 3; like terms; metric prefixes) are `numeric` and are refused on any slide whose text has a digit.
* Ambiguity vetoes (`avoid` / `requires` / families): e.g. RHS/SAS/SSS need a triangle context (not an equation's RHS), "exponential form" is index notation not standard form, "cubic" needs "graph" (not cubic centimetres), a cone slide naming the cylinder shows both, all four congruence criteria named together give no picture, the 8 circle-theorem pictures are one family, "without a unit circle" vetoes the unit circle, blood pressure vetoes the pressure triangle, "equal bar width" vetoes the histogram.
* Geometry is computed, never eyeballed: circle-theorem points on the circle by angle, tangents by acos(r/OP), angle marks from the real edge directions, regular polygons from the circumradius, frustum/cone by similar triangles, sector by exact angles, cumulative-frequency read-offs solved on the curve, Venn regions by clip-paths.

### Verification tables (X2 pictures)

Columns as in section 4. Oak evidence = the keyword definition found in `scratch/oak-raw` (KS3/KS4 Maths); where no Oak keyword has the exact name, the standard GCSE result is stated in the verification column.

| Picture id | Concepts (exact) | Subjects | Oak evidence (keyword definition found in scratch/oak-raw) | Drawing verification | Does NOT show |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `cubic-graph` | `cubic graph`, `cubic function`, `cubic curve` | Maths | no Oak keyword with this exact name; the phrase “cubic graph” is in 3 Oak lesson titles/keyword lists | Oak (KS4) keyword 'cubic': an equation, graph or sequence whose highest exponent is 3. Drawn: y = x³ − 3x; roots at 0 and ±√3 (exact), turning points at (−1, 2) and (1, −2) from the derivative 3x² − 3 = 0. | a negative x³ term (mirror image); a cubic with fewer than 3 roots; the equation |
| `reciprocal-graph` | `reciprocal graph`, `reciprocal function`, `reciprocal curve` | Maths | no Oak keyword with this exact name; the phrase “reciprocal graph” is in 3 Oak lesson titles/keyword lists | Oak KS4 lessons 'Drawing reciprocal graphs' / 'Key features of a reciprocal graph'; Oak keyword 'asymptote': a line a curve approaches but never touches. Drawn: y = 1/x for /x/ from 0.42 to 4.6; the axes are the asymptotes. | y = k/x with negative k; the equation; translations of the graph |
| `exponential-graph` | `exponential graph`, `exponential function`, `exponential curve`, `exponential growth`, `exponential decay` | Maths | no Oak keyword with this exact name; the phrase “exponential graph” is in 2 Oak lesson titles/keyword lists | Oak KS4 keyword 'exponential': general form y = ab^x (a the coefficient, b the base). Drawn: a = 1, y = 1.7^x (growth, b > 1) and y = 1.7^(−x) (decay, 0 < b < 1); both pass through (0, 1) (gold dot) and approach y = 0. | values on the axes; a ≠ 1; negative b |
| `y-mx-c` | `y = mx + c`, `equation of a line`, `equation of a straight line`, `equation of the line`, `straight line equation` | Maths | **equation of a line**: “An equation of a line is any equation whose graph forms a straight line.” (Finding the equation of the line ay + b…)<br>**equation of a straight line**: “An equation of a line is any equation whose graph forms a straight line. These equations can be written in th…” (Shapes on coordinate grids) | Oak KS3 Y8 lesson 'Finding the equation of the line y = mx + c'. m = change in y ÷ change in x (rise ÷ run); c = y-intercept (Oak keyword 'intercept': where a line meets an axis). The rise/run triangle lies exactly on the drawn line. | negative gradients; other forms of the equation of a line; numbers |
| `horizontal-vertical-lines` | `vertical and horizontal graphs`, `horizontal and vertical graphs`, `vertical and horizontal lines`, `horizontal and vertical lines`, `vertical graph`, `horizontal graph` | Maths | no Oak keyword with this exact name; the phrase “vertical and horizontal graphs” is in 2 Oak lesson titles/keyword lists | Oak KS4 lesson 'Drawing vertical and horizontal graphs'. Every point on a horizontal line has the same y-value (y = b); every point on a vertical line has the same x-value (x = a). Drawn: exactly horizontal and exactly vertical lines. | the values of a and b; diagonal lines |
| `direct-proportion-graph` | `direct proportion graph`, `graph of direct proportion`, `graphing direct proportion`, `graphs showing direct proportion`, `graph showing direct proportion` | Maths | no Oak keyword with this exact name; the phrase “direct proportion graph” is in 2 Oak lesson titles/keyword lists | Oak keyword 'direct proportion': two variables with a constant multiplicative relationship, so y = kx. The graph is a straight line through the origin (0 gives 0), gradient k. Drawn: line from the origin. | the value of k; inverse proportion |
| `inverse-proportion-graph` | `inverse proportion graph`, `graph of inverse proportion`, `graphs showing inverse proportion`, `graph showing inverse proportion` | Maths | no Oak keyword with this exact name; the phrase “inverse proportion graph” is in 2 Oak lesson titles/keyword lists | Oak keyword 'inverse proportion': a constant multiplicative relationship between one variable and the reciprocal of the other, so y = k/x. For positive x and k the graph is a decreasing curve approaching, but never touching, the axes. Drawn: y = 1.6/x. | negative x-values (the other branch); the value of k; direct proportion |
| `speed-time-graph` | `speed-time graph`, `speed time graph`, `velocity-time graph`, `velocity time graph` | Maths | no Oak keyword with this exact name; the phrase “speed-time graph” is in 3 Oak lesson titles/keyword lists | Oak KS4 lesson 'Speed-time graphs': the gradient of a speed-time graph is the acceleration (Oak: acceleration is the rate of change of speed) and the area under it is the distance travelled. Drawn: straight-line segments up, flat and down. | curved (non-uniform acceleration) graphs; values on the axes |
| `unit-circle` | `unit circle`, `sine function`, `cosine function` | Maths | **sine function**: “The sine of an angle (sin(θ°)) is the y-coordinate of point P on the triangle formed inside the unit circle.” (The unit circle)<br>**cosine function**: “The cosine of an angle (cos(θ°)) is the x-coordinate of point P on the triangle formed inside the unit circle.” (The unit circle) | Oak KS3/KS4 lessons 'The unit circle' and 'Scaling the right-angled triangle from the unit circle'. On the unit circle, the radius to P makes angle θ with the x-axis and P = (cos θ, sin θ). Drawn: P at 40 degrees, exact coordinates from the circle. | angles beyond 90 degrees; the tangent line; specific values |
| `circle-equation` | `equation of a circle`, `equation of the circle`, `circle equation` | Maths | no Oak keyword with this exact name; the phrase “equation of a circle” is in 1 Oak lesson titles/keyword lists | Pythagoras' theorem on the right-angled triangle formed by the radius, x and y gives x² + y² = r² for a circle centred on the origin (standard GCSE result). Drawn: the point at 36 degrees exactly on the circle. | circles not centred on the origin; numbers |
| `gradient-of-curve` | `gradient of a curve`, `gradient of the curve`, `tangent to a curve`, `tangent to the curve` | Maths | no Oak keyword with this exact name; the phrase “gradient of a curve” is in 2 Oak lesson titles/keyword lists | Oak KS4 lessons 'Estimating the gradient of a curve' / 'Improving the estimate of the gradient of a curve': the gradient at a point is the gradient of the tangent there; a chord to a nearby point gives an estimate. Drawn: y = 90((x−30)/170)² with its tangent at x = 112 (slope computed exactly). | numerical gradients; calculus notation |
| `graph-transformations` | `transforming graphs`, `graph transformation`, `transformations of graphs`, `transformation of graphs` | Maths | no Oak keyword with this exact name; the phrase “transforming graphs” is in 7 Oak lesson titles/keyword lists | Standard GCSE results for a > 0: f(x) + a translates up by a; f(x + a) translates left by a; −f(x) reflects in the x-axis; f(−x) reflects in the y-axis (Oak KS4 unit 'Transformations of graphs'). Drawn with the asymmetric curve exp(−1.6(x − 0.6)²) so each change is unambiguous. | stretches; combined transformations; numerical values of a |
| `graph-stretches` | `y = af(x)`, `y = f(ax)`, `stretching graphs`, `graph stretch` | Maths | no Oak keyword with this exact name; the phrase “y = af(x)” is in 1 Oak lesson titles/keyword lists | Standard GCSE results: y = af(x) is a stretch parallel to the y-axis, scale factor a; y = f(ax) is a stretch parallel to the x-axis, scale factor 1/a (Oak KS4 'Transforming graphs: y = af(x)', 'y = f(ax)'). Drawn for a = 1.6 and a = 2 on the same base curve. | translations and reflections; 0 < a < 1 |

### X2 verification run
* `cd server && npx tsx src/oak/factory/art/cli.ts check`: 0 problems (includes 673 X2 tests: for every picture a positive slide that must get exactly that picture at KS3 and KS4, the same slide must NOT get it at KS1/KS2, a practice slide must get none, a negative slide (gate / look-alike / passing mention) must not, numeric pictures refused with digits; plus family, multi-picture and owner-example tests).
* `npx tsx src/oak/factory/cli.ts check --subject Maths --keystage ks3|ks4`: 0 invalid.
* Contact sheets of all 110 pictures viewed in light and dark theme (`scratch` helper renders; positions of labels, overlaps and caption clashes fixed by eye).
* Real slides: 40 lessons (142 slides carrying an X2 picture) were imported to the STAGING tenant only (`import.ts pnH8zTuvYlb7yJbvcanr --slides --lesson <slug>`, all 40 OK). The Playwright Preview run (`e2e/x2-art-sheet.spec.ts`, a copy of the F1 sheet spec reading `scratch/x2-art-samples.json`, via `scripts/e2e-locked.sh`) could NOT complete: the machine's disk was 98% full (`No space left on device`, Turbopack cache write failures) and the dev web server returned 404 for `/login`. Instead the real generated slides (title + every text block exactly as generated, next to the picture SVG in the real theme CSS) were rendered in Playwright/Chromium and I looked at ~70 of them (24 sheets of 6): see "Contradictions found and fixed" below. The spec is ready to run when the dev stack is healthy: `scripts/e2e-locked.sh e2e/x2-art-sheet.spec.ts` (X2_LIMIT, X2_THEME=light|dark, X2_WIDTH=390).

### Contradictions / mismatches found on real slides and fixed
* HCF/LCM prime-factor Venn appeared on "Highest common factor with algebraic terms" (wrong: it says prime factors of two numbers) -> vetoed by `algebraic` / `expression`.
* Like-terms picture said "same letters" but a lesson covers exponents -> caption now "same letters with the same powers".
* Frustum picture said "volume =" on a surface-area lesson -> now "volume or curved surface area = big cone - small cone" (true for both).
* Cone picture lacked the base area on a surface-area slide (pi r l + pi r squared) -> added "base area = pi r squared".
* Plural titles ("Surface area of cylinders") missed the formula picture and got a redundant plain cylinder -> plural variants added for cylinder, cone, sphere, pyramid.
* Triangular prism picture appeared on a cylinder key-word slide (mentions "cross sections") -> `prism` vetoed by `cylinder`.
* Single stem-and-leaf diagram appeared on "Back-to-back stem and leaf" -> vetoed; population/sample picture appeared on "Stem and leaf ... single sample" -> vetoed by `stem and leaf`.
* "Equidistant" (a reflection key word) pulled the loci picture onto "Describing a reflection" -> `equidistant` removed from the loci concepts.
* Unit circle on "Using a calculator without a unit circle" -> vetoed by "without a unit circle".
* SSS/SAS/ASA/AAS/RHS pictures were vetoed by the UNIT title "similarity and Pythagoras" (prefix `similar`) -> `=similar` whole word only.
* "tan" in `avoid` matched every "tangent" (tangent-and-radius picture never appeared) -> `=tan`.

### Honest limits
* Coverage is 26.5% of all Maths KS3-4 slides (36.4% of non-question slides), not 100%: pictures exist only where a generic diagram of the slide's key concept is provably correct. Algebra-manipulation, number-arithmetic and worded problem lessons (most of Y7-Y9 arithmetic, simultaneous equations by elimination, iteration, algebraic fractions, functions of proof) have no honest generic diagram and correctly stay text-only.
* Some pictures are correct but tangential when the concept is only a key word of a multi-word Key words slide (e.g. the HCF/LCM Venn on a fraction lesson whose key words list "LCM").
* 7 pictures are not used by any current Oak slide (function-machine, frequency-polygon, quadrilateral-angle-sum, exterior-angle-triangle, pyramid-formula, net-triangular-prism, net-pyramid): correct and tested, kept for future lessons.
* Unit-circle and trigonometry: "sine function"/"cosine function" (Oak: y/x-coordinate on the unit circle) use the unit-circle picture; "tangent function" has no picture.

## X4 — English KS1-KS4: 134 new teaching-diagram pictures

Author: Agent X4 (overnight run, 2026-09-20). Scope: English (2,671 lessons, 19,114 generated slides). Files: `server/src/oak/factory/art/ext-english.ts` (+ `ext-english-kit.ts`, `-a` grammar/punctuation, `-b` word level/phonics, `-c` text types/reading skills/handwriting, `-d` literary devices/poetry/drama, `-tests.ts`), registered by one line in `art/library.ts`; unit tests wired into `art/cli.ts check`.

### X4.1 Measurement (before)
English had **207 of 19,114 slides (1.1%; 1.5% of the 13,380 non-question slides) with a picture, in 108 of 2,670 lessons (4.0%)** — only the story mountain, handwriting lines and a few literal emoji. Where the pictures were missing: the concepts on English slides are grammar terms, literary devices, text types and reading skills. Ranking of key-word terms/titles/leads by slides affected (before): adjective 358, suffix 324, noun 269, paragraph 246, non-chronological report 166, fronted adverbial 155, tense 142, clause 136, complex sentence 129, imagery 125, root word 117, persuasive 113, essay 112, diary entry 107, subheading 105, homophone 105, recount 93, rhyme scheme 41 ... Rejected candidates of the 9 old English pictures (1,510 slides): 734 + 609 were Maths/Science pictures whose word also occurs in English ("area", "volume", "reflection"), 215 not prominent, 17 missing the context word, 5 vetoed. So the gap was not the policy but the missing English diagrams.

### X4.2 What was drawn
134 NEW pictures, all **generic teaching diagrams** of the concept (definition card / frame / colour key) with neutral invented examples; none depicts a specific book, character, poem or event, none is a guessed illustration of a text. Groups: word classes (noun, common/proper noun, abstract noun, verb, adjective, adverb, pronoun, preposition, conjunction, determiner, word-class key, singular/plural, comparatives, modal and auxiliary verbs), tense (timeline, progressive, perfect, active/passive), sentences (simple, compound, complex, relative clause, clause vs phrase, expanded noun phrase, fronted adverbial, sentence structures, the four sentence functions), punctuation (overview, full stop, question mark, exclamation mark, comma, colon, semi-colon, hyphen, brackets/dashes, apostrophe x4, direct speech, reported speech, capital letters), morphology and phonics (prefix, suffix, root word, prefix un-, suffixes -ed/-ing/-ly/-ful/-less/-ment,-ness, y to ies, doubling the consonant, compound words, synonym, antonym, homophone, homonym, word family, syllable, digraph, trigraph, split digraph, phoneme/grapheme, vowels/consonants, short/long vowels, silent letters, soft c, rhyme, etymology, dictionary entry), text types and structure (paragraph, essay/thesis, point-evidence-explain, non-chronological report, recount, diary, letter, newspaper article, instructions, persuasive writing, debate, writing process, story elements, five-part story structure), reading skills (characterisation, show-don't-tell, first/third person, inference, retrieve, summarise, predict, compare/contrast, cohesive devices, imagery, figurative language, speaking skills), literary devices (simile, metaphor, extended metaphor, personification, alliteration, onomatopoeia, hyperbole, pathetic fallacy, oxymoron, irony, dramatic irony, foreshadowing, flashback, symbolism, motif, juxtaposition, anaphora, rhetorical question, repetition, semantic field, kenning), poetry and drama (stanza/quatrain, rhyme scheme, free verse, sonnet, haiku, enjambment, caesura, refrain, soliloquy, play script/stage directions, tragic hero arc), handwriting (tramlines, lead in/lead out).

### X4.3 Safety rules used (so a picture cannot contradict a slide)
* Exact whole-phrase `concepts` only; the concept must be prominent (title / key-word term / lead, or lesson title + twice) — the existing policy.
* `requires` for polysemous concepts (`plan`, `setting`, `plot`, `statement`, `command`, `pitch`, `volume`, `comparative`, `lead in`, `tragedy`) and `avoid` vetoes for the other senses found by reading real slides: e.g. `comparative` needs adjective/adverb/suffix context (so "comparative imagery" and "comparative paragraphs" get nothing); `tense` is vetoed by "nervous/worried/atmosphere"; `lead in/out` is vetoed by "no lead-ins" lessons; `sentence structure` is vetoed by declarative/exclamatory/inversion slides; `summary` (the slide title) never triggers the summarise picture; the speaking skills picture is vetoed for fiction/reading; a picture for an exact affix (`un-`, `-ed`, `-ing`, `-ly`, `-ful`, `-less`, `-ment/-ness`) is vetoed when the slide names other prefixes; `metaphor` never shows on "extended metaphor"; `irony` never shows on "dramatic irony"; `hyphen`/`dash`, `singular`/`plural possession` are kept apart.
* Never on question slides; max 2 per slide; quoted book titles are removed before matching (the existing policy).
* Letter-specific pictures (formation of a named letter) were NOT drawn: without a per-letter concept phrase on the slide they would be guesses.
* Bug fixed in `art/select.ts` (one line): `chooseArt` built its veto text only when the NORMALISED lesson title was non-empty, but `verifyArt` when the raw lesson title was; for a lesson whose title is only a quoted book title (`'Hickory Dickory Dock'`) the unit title (`reading poetry`) vetoed in verifyArt but not in chooseArt, which invalidated 3 curated decks. Both now use the raw lesson title.

### X4.4 Results
| | Before | After (this library, all 134 pictures) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| English slides with a picture | 207 of 19,114 (1.1%; 1.5% of the 13,380 non-question slides) | **5,379 of 16,408 (32.8%)** = **40.2% of the 13,380 non-question slides** (question slides never get one). The generator now writes 16,408 English slides instead of 19,114 (another agent changed `generate.ts` while I worked: 2,700 fewer practice/check slides); the 13,380 teaching slides are unchanged |
| English lessons with at least one picture | 108 of 2,670 (4.0%) | **1,994 of 2,670 (74.7%)** |
| Slides with two pictures | few | 1,462 |
| Question slides with a picture | 0 | 0 (by rule) |
The remaining ~60% of teaching slides have no picture by design: literary set-text analysis slides (themes, context, character analysis of a named text) have no generic diagram that cannot contradict them.

### X4.5 Verification
* `cd server && npx tsx src/oak/factory/art/cli.ts check` -> 0 problems, including 622 X4 tests (per picture: positive, passing-mention, question-slide, avoid-veto, wrong-subject; plus 24 hand-written regression tests for the contradictions found in review) and a layout check (no label leaves the canvas).
* `cd server && npx tsx src/oak/factory/cli.ts check` -> INVALID 0 for English (all 2,671 English lessons valid); the 3 invalid curated decks that appeared with the first draft were the `select.ts` inconsistency above and are fixed.
* Reviewed by eye: light and dark contact sheets of all 134 pictures; ~380 real assigned slides read in text form (per picture 3-10 random samples, all high-volume pictures twice) which found and removed 9 contradiction classes (see X4.3).
* `npx tsc --noEmit` root and server clean (only the known `_check_e2.ts` error).
* Rendered check: the 40 sample lessons (114 slides with a picture + every 4th plain slide) were imported to the STAGING tenant only (`import.ts pnH8zTuvYlb7yJbvcanr --slides --lesson <slug>`, list in `scratch/x4-art-samples.json`) and `e2e/x4-art-sheet.spec.ts` (a copy of `f1-art-sheet.spec.ts` for these samples; run only via `scripts/e2e-locked.sh e2e/x4-art-sheet.spec.ts`) asserts exact picture ids, alt text, inline svg, no empty panel, no overflow and screenshots every slide to `scratch/x4-art-shots/`. **That run could NOT complete**: it waited ~40 min for the e2e lock behind other agents (machine load average > 600), then three attempts failed in Playwright's `webServer` step (web dev server did not answer within 120 s; later `localhost:3000` served HTTP 404 for every route from a dev server started at 14:01 by someone else, so Playwright refused to reuse it). I did not touch that server. **The lead should run the spec once the stack is healthy.** As a fallback I rendered 80 real generated slides (the real slide text next to the exact verified picture(s), the same SVG the app inlines, light/dark theme variables) with a standalone Chromium page and read 70 of them against their pictures (nothing contradicted; the `-ful and -less` slide, `ABBA` rhyme scheme, `prefixes un-, non-, dis-, mis-` and `letter writing` cases were found there and fixed).

### X4.7 Not done / honest limits
* Handwriting letter-FORMATION pictures (start point + arrows per named letter/group, "formation of b and p") were not drawn: Oak's handwriting slides name letters in quoted lists / lesson titles, so a formation picture would need per-letter concepts and a stroke-order I could not verify against the Oak scheme; those slides keep the generic `handwriting-lines`, `tramlines`, `lead-in-out` and `capital-letters` pictures.
* Set-text analysis slides (Macbeth, poems, novels) only get a picture when the slide teaches a device or form (simile, sonnet, soliloquy, pathetic fallacy ...); their themes/context/characters correctly get none.
* The reading-skills and text-type pictures are generic frames (e.g. point-evidence-explain, five-part story structure); a slide from a course that teaches a differently named frame is not contradicted, only not matched.
* `phonics` grapheme lessons quoted as 'ay' / 'igh' cannot match a picture: quoted text is removed by the normaliser (existing policy).

## 8. X5 — French / Spanish / German: verified slide images (overnight run, 2026-09-20)

Author: Agent X5. Scope: the 1,366 language lessons (French 492, Spanish 492, German 382; KS2-KS4 = 9,870 generated slides, 7,365 teaching slides). Owner: "ITS INCREDIBLY IMPORTANT THAT THE IMAGE DOESNT CONTRADICT THE PAGE OR CONTENTS SO DO YOUR RESEARCH FOR EVERY IMAGE." The policy is unchanged (a picture only when a verified match exists; a wrong picture is far worse than none); X5 only widened what can be verified.

### 8.1 Before / after (final code, `npx tsx src/oak/factory/cli.ts check --subject <lang>`: 0 invalid in all three)

| | Before X5 | After X5 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Slides with a picture, French | 24 of 3,507 (0.7%) | **584 of 3,507 (16.7%)** = 571 diagram slides + 14 emoji slides (teaching slides: 22.0%) |
| Slides with a picture, Spanish | 34 of 3,567 (1.0%) | **510 of 3,567 (14.3%)** = 490 diagram slides + 24 emoji slides (teaching slides: 19.2%) |
| Slides with a picture, German | 18 of 2,796 (0.6%) | **302 of 2,796 (10.8%)** = 289 diagram slides + 16 emoji slides (teaching slides: 14.7%) |
| All three | 76 of 9,870 (0.8%) | **1,396 of 9,870 (14.1%)**; teaching slides 19.0% |
| Lessons with at least one picture | 64 of 1,366 | 702 of 1,366 (51.4%) |
| Card emoji (title is itself a listed noun) | 132 | 149 |
| Pictures on practice / check slides | 0 | 0 (rule + validator unchanged) |

Why not more: Oak's MFL lessons are grammar lessons (a slide is titled "Using -er verbs in the present", not "the dog"), so concrete nouns are rare in prominent positions (5,969 of 7,365 teaching slides still carry no picture). (slides with both a diagram and an emoji are counted once) The largest rejection reasons on the slides that still have no picture: another tense or topic named on the slide/lesson/unit title vetoes a tense-specific picture (about 2,920 slide-candidates), the concept is only mentioned in passing (about 1,550), a "requires" context word is missing (about 345: German case table needs "article", Spanish regular preterite needs "-ar/-er/-ir/regular"). Words Oak puts in single quotes ('faire', 'la pomme') are removed by the shared normaliser (it strips quoted titles for English) so quoted vocabulary is never matched: a deliberate consequence, not changed.

### 8.2 Measured gaps (ranked by slides affected; from the final decks)

Key-word terms on slides that still have no picture (count of slides): French — preposition 17, adjective 15, adverb 12, comparative 10, liaison 9, intonation 9, emphatic pronoun 9, sound-symbol correspondence 8, imperfect tense 7, conditional 7, imperative 7, aller + infinitive 7, simple future 7, negation 6, modal verb 5; Spanish — preterite 45, simple present 20, estar 11, present continuous 11, imperfect 11, personal 'a' 8, subjunctive 7, comparative 5; German — dative 19, preposition 18, perfect tense 14, adverb 13, modal verb 13, imperfect tense 13, open question 12, accusative 12, strong verb 11, werden 9, separable verb 6, word order two/three 7/6. Reasons: (1) tense pictures are vetoed when another tense is named (deliberately), (2) Spanish preterite/imperfect need the regular -ar/-er/-ir context (an irregular preterite must never get the regular table), (3) parts of speech (preposition, adverb, adjective, liaison, SSC) have no single depiction I could verify, (4) subjunctive, present continuous, dative verbs, separable verbs and the imperative were not drawn (irregular or context-dependent: not verifiable as generic-correct).

### 8.3 What was built (files)

* `server/src/oak/factory/art/ext-languages.ts` — 133 new SVG diagrams (registered by one line in `library.ts`): present-tense tables for 41 high-frequency verbs (17 French, 9 Spanish, 15 German; complete six-person forms, only the verb named, present tense only), regular -er / -ar / -er / -ir / weak-verb tables with highlighted endings, model-verb tables for the imperfect (fr, es, de), simple future (fr, es), conditional (fr, es), Spanish preterite (regular -ar and -er/-ir side by side); sentence frames with neutral example words (French ne … pas, aller + infinitive, perfect tense structure, pronoun position, modal verb + infinitive, il y a, yes/no question forms, comparatives; Spanish no + verb, ir a + infinitive, perfect, pronoun position, gustar, modal, hay, yes/no questions, comparatives; German verb-second (WO2), verb-final after weil (WO3), time-manner-place, perfect, werden + infinitive, modal, es gibt, yes/no questions, comparatives); question-word cards (qui/que/où …, ¿quién?/¿dónde? …, wer/was/wo …); article + gender tables (French, Spanish, German nominative), German cases table (der/den/dem), French partitive, subject-pronoun tables, reflexive-pronoun tables, possessive adjectives, regular adjective agreement (French grand/grande, Spanish alto/alta), infinitive and regular past-participle pictures; numbers 1-12 and 13-31, days, months, seasons, colours (real colour swatches), accents/umlauts/special letters, and telling-the-time (four clocks drawn exactly: 3:00, 3:15, 3:30, 3:45; German "halb vier" is drawn as 3:30) in all three languages; flags for Haiti, Mexico (no coat of arms, stated), Peru, Chile, Senegal, Morocco (only when the slide names that exact country, same veto lists as the existing flags plus history words).
* `server/src/oak/factory/art/ext-languages-allow.ts` — literal-emoji extension (registered by one call at the bottom of `allowlist.ts`): 117 new emoji entries (concrete nouns) in French/Spanish/German plus English glosses (animals, food & drink, clothes, school objects, home, places in town, transport, sport, weather, family/people) and German plurals / French -x plurals / Spanish -es plurals and French elided forms (l'oiseau). Merges into existing entries (never duplicates an emoji). Removes two base words that are also verb forms: French `porte` (elle porte = she wears) and `lit` (il lit = he reads). New `EMOJI_AVOID` vetoes: chat (conversation), in pain, pen pal, tongue twister, mother tongue, school trip / report, sound/pronunciation lessons (SSC titles such as "[o/au/eau]" quote a spelling, not the noun).
* `server/src/oak/factory/art/ext-languages-tests.ts` — 70 policy tests (positive AND negative, false friends), imported by `cli.ts`; `ext-languages-cli.ts langcheck` — the second-read tool (below); `e2e/x5-languages-sheet.spec.ts` + `scratch/x5-languages-samples.json` — rendered check of real slides.
* Shared-file changes (small): `art/select.ts` — **concept scanning is now subject-aware** (`picKeys(subject)`). Before, the scan was subject-blind: when a phrase such as "negation", "days of the week", "comparative" or "reflection" belonged to pictures of several subjects/languages, the first picture in the list consumed the phrase and the picture of the right subject/language never got a hit (a Spanish "negation" slide could only ever match the French picture and be vetoed). `art/cli.ts`: one import + tests loop, and one existing test expectation ("French flag never for the plain word French") now expects the new `fr-perfect` picture instead of nothing (still never a flag). `library.ts` / `allowlist.ts`: one registration line each.

### 8.4 Rules I applied to every picture (why none can contradict its slide)

* Generic-correct: tables show the exact verb / paradigm named, complete and standard; model verbs are labelled "model verb" / "regular verb"; frames use neutral words. Tense pictures veto any other tense named on the slide OR in the lesson/unit title (perfect vs imperfect vs future vs conditional vs imperative vs preterite); irregular-form vetoes (fui/fue/hizo/tuvo …) and `requires` (regular/-ar/-er/-ir) protect the Spanish preterite/imperfect; adjective agreement is vetoed for "adjectives ending in -e / -eux / irregular"; the German nominative article table is vetoed by accusative/dative/genitive/case, the German case table needs "article(s)"; `sein` the verb is vetoed on the possessive `sein`.
* Note the quoted-word blind spot: because quoted words vanish from the matched text, vetoes written for a quoted verb (e.g. 'être') do not fire; therefore tense-generic captions were written so they stay true for every verb ("imperfect: stem + these endings", not "nous stem"), and no picture claims a rule that an irregular verb breaks.
* Modal-verb frames are tied to the phrase "modal verb" only (not "two-verb structure"): Oak's two-verb structures also take participles and gerunds, and the frame says "the second verb is an infinitive".
* Language accuracy: every foreign word was written from standard grammar and then checked by `ext-languages-cli.ts langcheck`, which looks up every word drawn in any picture in the vocabulary of Oak's own raw text for that language (French 148,659 words, Spanish 119,514, German 86,003). Result: 2,586 distinct drawn words, 63 not in Oak's text; every one re-read by hand: 2nd-person-plural and rarer forms that Oak never prints (parliez, parlaient, parleras …, hablabais, comíais, hablarían, müsst, dürft, spieltest, spieltet, wäschst, hacéis, queréis, laváis), the words tréma / diéresis / güe / güi / A-Umlaut / Bär / lila / vivido, and English labels (Friday, Feb, Sep, masc., purple, owned …). All spelled correctly with accents.
* False friends handled by the tests: fr chat (cat) vs English "chat", German Chat (no cat), fr porte / il lit, es metro (metre), sound-lesson spellings (eau, ei), school trip vs school building, tongue twister, German halb vier = 3:30, Eiffel Tower is NOT 🗼 (Tokyo Tower; not used), fr avocat / es langosta / es banco / fr souris / lengua / règle left out, German Eis / Bein / Laden left out.

### 8.5 Verification run

* `cd server && npx tsx src/oak/factory/art/cli.ts check` → 741 pictures at the time of the last run (all agents), 0 problems (policy tests: F1 + X1..X5 all pass). `npx tsx src/oak/factory/cli.ts check --subject French|Spanish|German` → INVALID 0 (492 / 492 / 382 decks). tsc root and server: only the pre-existing `_check_` error.
* Contact sheets of all 133 pictures viewed in light and dark; layout bugs found and fixed by eye: table captions colliding with the last row (12 pictures), overlapping columns in the possessive and subject-pronoun tables, invisible colour swatches (CSS `.l{fill:none}` overrode the fill), title text overflowing, accents row labels.
* Real slides: 46 lessons (chosen by greedy cover so every picture id appears) were imported to the STAGING tenant only (`pnH8zTuvYlb7yJbvcanr`, targeted `--slides --lesson <slug>`), played in Preview by `e2e/x5-languages-sheet.spec.ts` (through `scripts/e2e-locked.sh`) and screenshotted into `scratch/x5-lang-shots/`; see 8.6 for what I saw.

### 8.6 Rendered review

* `e2e/x5-languages-sheet.spec.ts` (locked run): 46 staging lessons queued; the shared dev stack was overloaded by the other agents' runs, so 17 of the 46 lessons timed out on navigation (`page.goto` / search click, no content failure) and 29 lessons were fully walked: **91 slides asserted, 64 with pictures (63 diagram slides + emoji) and 27 without; 0 mismatches between the pictures the generator chose and the pictures rendered (exact ids, alt text present, inline svg, no art panel and no empty box on the 27 plain slides, no page errors).** Screenshots: `scratch/x5-lang-shots/*.png`. I looked at all 64 picture slides (contact sheets) and 8 plain slides.
* What I saw and fixed by eye: `¿cuánto?` was glossed only "how much" on a "cuántos / cuántas" lesson (now "how much / many"); two stacked tables on a Key-words slide with several verbs make a tall panel (allowed, each is correct for a named term); the German case-table header columns collided (moved).
* Judgement calls I left in (correct but tangential, none contradicts): a Spain flag on the "compass points in Spain" slide; a "the infinitive" picture on a slide that only says "aller plus an infinitive"; conjugation tables on "Key words" slides that list up to two verbs; the family emoji card on "family" lessons.
* Limits: text in the tables is small (9.5-10.5 px at the 240 px panel width); the panel layout is shared (SlideDeck) and I did not change it. Phone width (390 px) and light theme were not re-run for X5 (the pictures use the same theme classes as F1's, and I viewed all contact sheets in both themes).
